codex
The older trail shows the original FC1 sequence was `gate` then `up` with activation in `gate`. v160’s E16 Accepted path changes both factors together: `up` then `gate`, and activation in `up`.

codex
**FACTORIAL_RATIONALE**

v160 bundled two E16 changes:

1. FC1 matmul order: `up` computed before `gate`.
2. Surviving storage: SwiGLU wrote into `up_output`, then `activation = up_output`.

v167 should isolate the complementary factor: keep the v160 E16 `up -> gate` compute order, but restore original surviving storage by writing SwiGLU into `gate_output`.

**SELECTED_EXPERIMENT**

E16 order-only ablation from v160:

```python
if num_experts == 16:
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
else:
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
```

E32/E64 retain v160 original paths exactly.

**MODIFICATION_POINTS**

Only edit [v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py](D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py:383) when creating `v167_e16_order_only.py`.

Change only the E16 branch:

```diff
 if num_experts == 16:
     up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
     gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
-    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, up_output)
-    del gate_output
-    activation = up_output
+    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
+    del up_output
+    activation = gate_output
```

**MATH_ALIAS_PROOF**

SwiGLU computes:

```text
activation = gate * sigmoid(gate) * up
```

The output buffer may alias `gate_output` or `up_output` because each element reads `gate_output[e,i,j]` and `up_output[e,i,j]` before writing `activation[e,i,j]`. There is no cross-element dependency. Writing into `gate_output` preserves the mathematical result as long as `up_output` is still alive during the kernel call, which it is.

So v167 is math-identical to v160 and parent behavior.

**SHAPE_ISOLATION**

Shape isolation is clean:

- `num_experts == 16`: changed to order-only.
- `num_experts == 32`: unchanged from v160.
- `num_experts == 64`: unchanged from v160.

This avoids repeating the v165 mistake of broadening the experiment across all shapes, and avoids the v166 E32 storage-only negative path.

**EXPECTED_SIGNAL**

Interpretation matrix:

```text
v167 E16 improves or matches v160:
  E16 gain likely comes mostly from FC1 compute order / allocator lifetime timing.

v167 E16 regresses toward parent:
  v160 gain likely came mostly from surviving storage into up_output.

v167 is noise-equivalent:
  v160鈥檚 -0.009 ms / -0.44% was probably fragile noise or allocator incidental.
```

Information value is high because this is the missing E16 factorial component after v160, v165, and v166. It should be the last nearby permutation in this family.

**IMPLEMENTATION**

Create `v167_e16_order_only.py` from `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, apply the three-line E16 branch edit above, and leave all other code untouched. I did not inspect `xpuoj/` or old `fused_moe_gemm/`.

**FINAL_GATE**

PASS_TO_IMPLEMENT
tokens used

1. FC1 matmul order: `up` computed before `gate`.
2. Surviving storage: SwiGLU wrote into `up_output`, then `activation = up_output`.

v167 should isolate the complementary factor: keep the v160 E16 `up -> gate` compute order, but restore original surviving storage by writing SwiGLU into `gate_output`.

**SELECTED_EXPERIMENT**

E16 order-only ablation from v160:

```python
if num_experts == 16:
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
else:
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
```

E32/E64 retain v160 original paths exactly.

**MODIFICATION_POINTS**

Only edit [v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py](D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py:383) when creating `v167_e16_order_only.py`.

Change only the E16 branch:

```diff
 if num_experts == 16:
     up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
     gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
-    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, up_output)
-    del gate_output
-    activation = up_output
+    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
+    del up_output
+    activation = gate_output
```

**MATH_ALIAS_PROOF**

SwiGLU computes:

```text
activation = gate * sigmoid(gate) * up
```

The output buffer may alias `gate_output` or `up_output` because each element reads `gate_output[e,i,j]` and `up_output[e,i,j]` before writing `activation[e,i,j]`. There is no cross-element dependency. Writing into `gate_output` preserves the mathematical result as long as `up_output` is still alive during the kernel call, which it is.

So v167 is math-identical to v160 and parent behavior.

**SHAPE_ISOLATION**

Shape isolation is clean:

- `num_experts == 16`: changed to order-only.
- `num_experts == 32`: unchanged from v160.
- `num_experts == 64`: unchanged from v160.

This avoids repeating the v165 mistake of broadening the experiment across all shapes, and avoids the v166 E32 storage-only negative path.

**EXPECTED_SIGNAL**

Interpretation matrix:

```text
v167 E16 improves or matches v160:
  E16 gain likely comes mostly from FC1 compute order / allocator lifetime timing.

v167 E16 regresses toward parent:
  v160 gain likely came mostly from surviving storage into up_output.

v167 is noise-equivalent:
  v160鈥檚 -0.009 ms / -0.44% was probably fragile noise or allocator incidental.
```

Information value is high because this is the missing E16 factorial component after v160, v165, and v166. It should be the last nearby permutation in this family.

**IMPLEMENTATION**

Create `v167_e16_order_only.py` from `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, apply the three-line E16 branch edit above, and leave all other code untouched. I did not inspect `xpuoj/` or old `fused_moe_gemm/`.

**FINAL_GATE**

PASS_TO_IMPLEMENT

codex
**FACTORIAL_RATIONALE**

v160 bundled two E16 changes:

1. FC1 matmul order: `up` computed before `gate`.
2. Surviving storage: SwiGLU wrote into `up_output`, then `activation = up_output`.

v167 should isolate the complementary factor: keep the v160 E16 `up -> gate` compute order, but restore original surviving storage by writing SwiGLU into `gate_output`.

**SELECTED_EXPERIMENT**

E16 order-only ablation from v160:

```python
if num_experts == 16:
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
else:
    gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
    up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
    del up_output
    activation = gate_output
```

E32/E64 retain v160 original paths exactly.

**MODIFICATION_POINTS**

Only edit [v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py](D:/C_data/EurakaOpts/contest_fused_moe_gemm/v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py:383) when creating `v167_e16_order_only.py`.

Change only the E16 branch:

```diff
 if num_experts == 16:
     up_output = packed_input @ up_w.transpose(1, 2)
     gate_output = packed_input @ gate_w.transpose(1, 2)
-    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, up_output)
-    del gate_output
-    activation = up_output
+    swiglu(gate_output, up_output, group_sizes, gate_output)
+    del up_output
+    activation = gate_output
```

**MATH_ALIAS_PROOF**

SwiGLU computes:

```text
activation = gate * sigmoid(gate) * up
```

The output buffer may alias `gate_output` or `up_output` because each element reads `gate_output[e,i,j]` and `up_output[e,i,j]` before writing `activation[e,i,j]`. There is no cross-element dependency. Writing into `gate_output` preserves the mathematical result as long as `up_output` is still alive during the kernel call, which it is.

So v167 is math-identical to v160 and parent behavior.

**SHAPE_ISOLATION**

Shape isolation is clean:

- `num_experts == 16`: changed to order-only.
- `num_experts == 32`: unchanged from v160.
- `num_experts == 64`: unchanged from v160.

This avoids repeating the v165 mistake of broadening the experiment across all shapes, and avoids the v166 E32 storage-only negative path.

**EXPECTED_SIGNAL**

Interpretation matrix:

```text
v167 E16 improves or matches v160:
  E16 gain likely comes mostly from FC1 compute order / allocator lifetime timing.

v167 E16 regresses toward parent:
  v160 gain likely came mostly from surviving storage into up_output.

v167 is noise-equivalent:
  v160閳ユ獨 -0.009 ms / -0.44% was probably fragile noise or allocator incidental.
```

Information value is high because this is the missing E16 factorial component after v160, v165, and v166. It should be the last nearby permutation in this family.

**IMPLEMENTATION**

Create `v167_e16_order_only.py` from `v160_e16_fc1_sequential_free.py`, apply the three-line E16 branch edit above, and leave all other code untouched. I did not inspect `xpuoj/` or old `fused_moe_gemm/`.

codex
C500 local correctness passed all three cases, but XPUOJ failed the sample-equivalent E16 case with a large single-element miss. That split reinforces that this is a fragile runtime/storage behavior, not an intentional arithmetic change.
exec
"C:\\Windows\\System32\\WindowsPowerShell\\v1.0\\powershell.exe" -Command 'Get-Content -Raw v167_codex_oj_recap.txt' in D:\C_data\EurakaOpts\contest_fused_moe_gemm


codex
**RESULT_CLASSIFICATION**  
`v167` is `WrongAnswer`, not a timing candidate. The reported `2.042 ms` is invalid for promotion because case1 failed correctness with max abs diff `0.1533203125`.

**ORDER_ONLY_EFFECT**  
The E16 order-only ablation is unsafe. Mathematically, `up -> gate` with SwiGLU stored into `gate_output` should match, but XPUOJ shows the runtime behavior is not storage/order invariant.

**ALLOCATOR_ALIAS_ANALYSIS**  
Yes, the changed BMM order can plausibly alter allocator/storage synchronization enough to cause deterministic corruption. `v167` allocates `up_output` first, `gate_output` second, writes activation into `gate_output`, then deletes `up_output`. If the Tilelang SwiGLU launch is not fully reflected in PyTorch’s allocator lifetime/stream tracking, the freed `up_output` storage can become reusable while the custom kernel or following work still depends on it. The local C500 pass plus OJ WA split is consistent with this kind of allocator/runtime sensitivity, not a math error.

**RETRY_DECISION**  
One unchanged retry is defensible only as confirmation because local C500 passed and this is the final factorial cell. It should not be treated as a normal optimization retry. If it fails again, close it permanently; if it passes, still classify it as fragile and inferior to `v160`.

**FACTORIAL_CONCLUSION**  
`v160` combined two E16 factors: `up -> gate` order and surviving `up_output` storage. `v167` isolates order-only and fails, so the accepted behavior of `v160` cannot be attributed to order alone. The surviving `up_output` storage is part of the correctness/lifetime mechanism under the changed order.

**BASELINE_DECISION**  
Keep `v160` as baseline. Do not promote `v167`.

**FINAL_VERDICT**  
`v167` is an invalid order-only ablation. The factorial result says E16 `up -> gate` requires the `up_output` survival pattern used by `v160`; original `gate_output` survival under that order is allocator/lifetime fragile and should be abandoned.
